Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

### Page 2

2

耕興為台灣電子產品測試及驗證服務龍頭廠商：耕興成立於 1986 年，以強制性檢
測服務起家 ， 目前為台灣最大民營電子產品測試及驗證服務實驗室 ， 集檢測 、 認證 、
技術服務與零件銷售為一體，提供電子產品 EMC/Safety 等專業認證及防磁零組
件銷售，為全球少數能執行 5G sub-6GHz (FR1)、5G mmWave (FR2)、Wi-Fi 6、
Wi-Fi 6E高速行動通訊 、 無線傳輸產品且第一家取得802.11ay 與 5G NR mmWave
產品檢測認證之公司，並於台灣 /大陸/美國/德國設立實驗室。以營收結構區分，
檢測與認證收入佔營收約 90%、被動元件銷售佔 10%。國際競爭對手包含
UL/BV/PCTEST/SGS/Intertek 等、地區型競爭對手為耀登/東研信超/華測檢測/譜
尼測試等。 
2Q26 財報優於預期：耕興 2Q26 EPS 3.07 元，優於預期的 2.87 元，5G 手機、
AI NB、 企業AP 等產品檢測訂單狀況維持穩定 ， 公司1H26 5G NR市佔率 23.97%、
FCC 高階網通市占率 47.3%，維持領導地位。 
3Q26 FCC 轉單效益開始小幅貢獻：由於 FCC 擬禁止中國實驗室驗證賣往美國的
產品，公司開始收到一些高階測試的轉單，但 FCC 尚未正式全面禁止，故現在貢
獻不多，仍在等FCC 正式決策，預計11 月會有結果，目前粗估若轉單順利會有共
2~6 億元的營收貢獻，整體 3Q26 營運平穩， 預估 3Q26 營收為 12.47 億元
(+0.4%QoQ，+10.7%YoY)，毛利率 46.8%，營業利益率 30.5%，稅後淨利 2.93
億元(-6.3%QoQ，+13.9%YoY)，稅後 EPS 2.88 元。

### Page 3

3

2027 年公司若受益轉單，營運有望小幅創高：FCC 未來擬禁止所有中國實驗室為
在美國使用的電子設備 (手機、PC 等) 進行測試，研究處認為2027 年開始公司有
望受益中國實驗室受限產生的轉單效應，公司在 2027 年已有擴產計畫，地點仍待
公告，若 NTN (非地面網路)需求如預期增加，有相關設備購買需求，明年 CAPEX
有望上看 4 億台幣，整體預期營運可小幅創高，預估 2026 年營收為 49.04 億元
(+9.1%YoY)， 毛利率46.7%， 營業利益率29.3%， 稅後淨利11.39 億元(+3.9%YoY)，
稅後 EPS 11.18 元，2027 年營收為 52.04 億元(+6.1%YoY)，毛利率 47.4%，營
業利益率 31.2%，稅後淨利 12.54 億元(+10.1%YoY)，稅後 EPS 12.31 元，以過
往現金股利配發率 80%來算，預計配發 9.0 元現金股利，換算目前殖利率 4.2%，
維持買進建議，目標價 246 元(20X 2027 EPS)。

## Document 222: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것삼성전자(005930) 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것삼성전자(005930) 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

기업분석 2026.10.30 www.daishin.com  
  Results Comment 
삼성전자 
(005930) 
 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것 
류형근

hyungkeun.ryu@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 500,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
231,000 
반도체업종

◼ 3분기 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 
◼ 주주환원은 시점의 문제일 뿐. 강화 방향성은 여전히 유효. 
◼ 메모리반도체 이익의 폭증이 가격의 매력을 유발. 비중확대 전략 유효.

매력적인 가격, 변하지 않은 주주환원 정책의 방향성 
목표주가와 매수의견 유지. 메모리반도체 주도 폭발적 이익 상승이 가격 (주
가)의 매력을 지속 유발하고 있고, 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향성에도 변한 것
은 없다는 판단. 절대적으로 싼 주가를 비중 확대의 기회로 삼을 필요. 
1. 주주환원, 시점의 문제 
금번 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 대외 불확실성이 큰 만큼, 
시장은 자사주 매입/소각과 같은 즉각적 주가 부양 정책의 발표를 기대.  
당사는 삼성전자의 주주환원이 “시점의 문제”라 생각. 총 주주환원의 규모도, 
차기 (2027-2029년) 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향에도 변한 것은 없다는 판
단. 시장이 기대하는 자사주 매입/소각은 우선주 중심으로 시행될 가능성이 
높으며, 시행 시, 그 규모는 25조원 내외일 것 (DPS 12,567원)으로 전망. 
2. 메모리반도체, 더 올라갈 것 
보수적 환율 전망 (2027년 평균 환율 1,300원 적용)에도, 2027년 메모리반도
체 영업이익은 608조원에 달할 것. 이익 전망의 상향 여력 또한 유효. 
1) 최대 생산, 최대 판매 
삼성전자는 금일 4Q26 DRAM 판매량 가이던스로 전분기 대비 10% 중후반 
성장 (q-q +18%로 추정)을 제시. 일부 투자자들은 높아진 기저 부담으로 
2027년 판매 성장이 제한될 것을 우려. 당사는 높아진 기저에도 2027년 
DRAM 판매가 전년대비 20% 상승할 것으로 전망. 
- 극단적 수급 불균형 속, 삼성전자는 “최대 생산, 최대 판매” 정책을 시행 
중. 삼성전자만의 강력한 Execution이 20% 대 성장을 유지시킬 원동력. 
- DRAM 1c 수율의 조기 개선 속, 범용 DRAM 양산에 DRAM 1c Capa가 일
부 투입될 것으로 예상. Bit 성장을 높일 수 있는 변화가 될 것. 
2) 열려 있는 가격의 상방 
1Q27 모바일 DRAM에서 Surprise가 발생할 것이라는 의견을 유지 (전분기 
대비 30% 이상 상승). 장기계약이 체결된 CSP향으로도 가격의 추가 상승 여
력이 존재한다는 점을 상기할 필요. CSP의 경우, 당장의 비용 부담보다 AI 시
장 선점을 우선시. 장기계약으로 형성된 가격 Band의 천장 (Ceiling) 또한 시
장 우려 대비 낮지 않음을 감안할 필요. 2027년 범용 DRAM ASP는 전년대비 
40% 중후반까지 상승할 가능성 (현 전망: 전년대비 34% 상승).  
3) HBM, 제품 경쟁력과 점유율의 동반 성장  
최근 삼성전자향으로 HBM4/HBM4e 추가 공급 요청이 쇄도하고 있는 것으
로 추정. 일부 고객은 연내 추가 공급 시 2027년 계약 가격을 선제 지급할 
의사를 표출. 제품 경쟁력이 회복되었음을 보여주는 증거라 생각. 
제품 경쟁력의 회복과 최대 Capa의 이점 속, 2027년은 HBM 점유율 1위를 
확보하는 기념비적 해가 될 것으로 전망 (매출액 기준 점유율 38% 예상).

KOSPI 5937.66

## Document 240: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대단기 부담_2a8cdc97

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대단기 부담_2a8cdc97.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

목표주가 2,000,000

현재주가 
(26.10.02) 
1,354,000 
제약업종

◼ 3Q26 컨센서스 부합, 4Q26 성장폭 확대 전망 
◼ 5공장 Ramp-up 지속, 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용)

3Q26 Preview: 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 전망 
3Q26 매출액 1조 3,380억원(+6.4% yoy, +1.3% qoq), 영업이익 5,890억원(-
7.0% yoy, +0.4% qoq, OPM 44.0%)으로 컨센서스에 부합하는 실적을 전
망함. 3분기 평균 원/달러 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락하고, 지난 5월 
파업에 따른 생산 차질 영향이 반영되나, 5공장 PPQ 물량의 매출 인식이 
시작되며 yoy, qoq 외형 성장은 이어질 것으로 예상함.  
파업 당시 생산이 지연된 물량은 3Q 중 추가 생산되어 4Q26부터 매출에 반
영될 전망임. 5공장은 다수 프로젝트의 PPQ가 진행 중이며, Ramp-up에 
따라 매출 기여도 점진적으로 확대될 것으로 예상함. 이에 4Q26에는 파
업 이연 물량 반영과 5공장 매출 확대에 따라 실적 성장폭이 확대될 전
망임. 2026년 연간 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 5조 5,310억원(+21.4% 
yoy), 2조 4,780억원(+19.8% yoy, OPM 44.8%)으로 전망함.

신규 수주 회복 기대, 중장기 성장 기반은 확대 중 
관세 관련 불확실성으로 둔화됐던 고객사와의 수주 논의가 재개되고 있음. 
10월 2일 유럽 소재 제약사와 935억원 규모의 CMO 계약을 추가 체결
했으며, 4Q26 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목함.  
PolyPeptide 인수는 연내 거래 종결을 목표로 진행 중이며, 인수 완료 
시 펩타이드 분야로 CDMO 포트폴리오가 확대될 전망임. 6 공장도 연내 
착공을 목표로 준비 중임. 
한편, 노사 리스크는 단기 불확실성 요인임. 노조는 10/26부터 3주간 2
차 총파업을 예고함. 1차 대비 예정된 파업 기간이 길어진 만큼 실제 파
업 진행 시 생산 차질 규모가 주요 변수임. 다만 파업 개시 전까지 협상 
가능성이 열려 있어, 향후 노사 협상 진행 경과를 확인할 필요가 있음.

## Document 271: Anthropic IPO前更新招股书凸显盈利压力：Q3营收近170亿美元、同比增11倍，净亏损扩大92%，算力承诺近9000亿

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic IPO前更新招股书凸显盈利压力：Q3营收近170亿美元、同比增11倍，净亏损扩大92%，算力承诺近9000亿.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

新招股书显⽰，今年前三季度， Anthropic 营收超 330 亿美元、同⽐增 12 倍；三季度运营费
⽤逾 175 亿美元、超过营收，同⽐增 2.9 倍，其中算⼒及基础设施⽀出增 3.8 倍⾄ 98.6 亿，同
⽐增速远超⼆季度的 48% ；三季度经营业绩由⼆季度盈利 9.7 亿美元转亏 5.67 亿；到三季度
末承诺⽀出 8950 亿美元，其中 4475 亿在 2030 年底前发⽣，将近 700 亿需此后⼀年内⽀付。
摘要：
Anthropic IPO 前更新的招股书显⽰，尽管 Claude ⺟公司营收继续⾼速增⻓，但算⼒成本和⻓期
基础设施投⼊也在急剧膨胀，第三季度经营业绩由盈转亏、净亏损同⽐扩⼤，近 9000 亿美元的
算⼒及基础设施承诺⽀出进⼀步凸显其盈利压⼒。
根据 Anthropic 周⼀向美国证券交易委员会（ SEC ）提交的修订版 S-1 招股书，公司第三季度营收
达到 169.56 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 1127% ；净亏损达到 232.91 亿美元，较上年同期的 121.38 亿美元

扩⼤约 92% 。截⾄ 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇及此后，公司已签订的计算能⼒采购及租赁协议承诺⽀出总额约为
8954 亿美元。
企业客⼾持续扩张，前三季度营收超 330 亿美元
Anthropic 第三季度营收达到 169.56 亿美元，较第⼆季度的 115.55 亿美元环⽐增⻓约 46.7% ，较
2025 年同期的 13.82 亿美元增⻓ 1127% 。这意味着公司单季营收已达到⼀年前的约 12.3 倍。
从前三季度整体表现来看， Anthropic 今年前九个⽉累计营收达到 332.42 亿美元，远⾼于去年同
期的 25.45 亿美元，增幅约为 12 倍。企业客⼾的持续扩张是推动收⼊增⻓的重要动⼒。
截⾄ 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇， Anthropic 过去 12 个⽉贡献⾄少 10 万美元收⼊的企业客⼾超过 1.19 万家，其中，
过去 12 个⽉贡献⾄少 100 万美元收⼊的客⼾超过 1900 家，贡献⾄少 1000 万美元收⼊的客⼾约
250 家。
不过，营收⾼速增⻓的同时， Anthropic 的净亏损也在扩⼤。第三季度净亏损为 232.91 亿美元，

同⽐扩⼤约 92% ，较第⼆季度净亏损额 663.09 亿美元环⽐收窄空昂孔明 65% ，但主要受到可转
换票据、衍⽣负债及 SAFE 协议公允价值变动等⾮现⾦项⽬影响，不能简单理解为公司核⼼经营
亏损同步收窄。
算⼒成本激增，经营业绩较⼆季度由盈转亏
营收增⻓的另⼀⾯，是 Anthropic 为训练和运⾏ AI 模型付出的成本迅速攀升。
修订版招股书显⽰， Anthropic 第三季度总运营费⽤达到 175.23 亿美元，较上年同期的 44.71 亿美
元增⻓约 292% ，已经超过当季 169.56 亿美元的营收。
其中，算⼒及基础设施⽀出达到 98.63 亿美元，较上年同期的 20.47 亿美元增⻓约 382% ，相当于
当季营收的约 58% 。相⽐之下，这⼀⽐例在第⼆季度约为 48% ，显⽰算⼒投⼊加速正在给利润
率带来压⼒。
受成本上升影响， Anthropic 第三季度经营亏损达到 5.67 亿美元，⽽第⼆季度公司曾实现 9.74 亿
美元经营利润。这也是公司在连续多个季度经营亏损后，于第⼆季度⾸次实现经营盈利，随后

⼜在第三季度重新陷⼊经营亏损。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

Anthropic 解释称，第三季度经营业绩由盈转亏，主要受到两⽅⾯因素影响：⼀是约 50 亿美元通
过短期承诺采购的算⼒成本，这类算⼒⽬前明显贵于多年期合同采购的同类算⼒；⼆是约 20 亿
美元与员⼯捐赠匹配计划有关的⾮现⾦费⽤。
不过，剔除股权激励、⾮现⾦慈善捐赠等项⽬后， Anthropic 第三季度调整后经营利润仍达到
24.63 亿美元，⾼于第⼆季度的 22.35 亿美元。但调整后经营利润率从第⼆季度的 19% 降⾄第三
季度的 15% ，反映出更昂贵的短期算⼒采购仍在侵蚀公司的利润空间。
这也意味着， Anthropic 虽然已经证明了 AI 产品具备强劲的商业化能⼒，但能否将营收增⻓持续
转化为经营利润，仍取决于算⼒成本下降的速度能否跟上业务扩张。
近 9000 亿美元算⼒承诺，近半将在 2030 年底前兑现
⽐单季度成本更引⼈关注的，是 Anthropic 已经锁定的⻓期算⼒和基础设施⽀出。
修订版招股书披露，截⾄ 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇及此后，公司签订的算⼒采购承诺和租赁协议总额约为 8954 亿

美元，其中绝⼤部分与计算能⼒及基础设施有关。
从⽀出时间表来看，约 4475 亿美元预计将在 2030 年 12 ⽉ 31 ⽇前兑现，近半数承诺⽀出将在未来
四年多内陆续落地；另有约 698 亿美元将在截⾄ 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇后的 12 个⽉内、即 2027 年 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇之前
⽀付。其余绝⼤部分承诺预计将在 2031 年⾄ 2045 年间兑现。
Anthropic 表⽰，随着计算需求⼤幅增⻓，公司正在从单纯依赖固定期限云计算协议，转向结合
直接租赁芯⽚、专⽤数据中⼼等⽅式的混合模式，希望通过建设⾃有专⽤基础设施，逐步以成
本更低的⻓期算⼒替代⾼价短期采购。
不过，这些承诺也意味着巨⼤的资⾦压⼒。公司在招股书中警告，部分算⼒采购协议包含最低
采购承诺和照付不议条款，即使实际使⽤量不⾜，公司仍可能需要⽀付相关费⽤。
更值得注意的是， Anthropic 的收⼊主要依赖客⼾实际使⽤量，并⾮全部来⾃⻓期合同承诺；⽽
算⼒和基础设施⽀出却往往需要提前多年锁定。如果客⼾需求增⻓不及预期，或者公司⽆法及

时将新增算⼒转化为收⼊，可能⾯临现⾦流压⼒，甚⾄难以履⾏相关合同义务。
截⾄ 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇， Anthropic 拥有约 1466 亿美元流动性，包括 956 亿美元现⾦及现⾦等价物、 363 亿
美元短期投资，以及 148 亿美元信贷额度可⽤资⾦。公司表⽰，未来将通过业务收⼊和融资筹集
资⾦⽀持相关投资。
IPO 估值⾯临重新审视， AI 商业模式接受考验
Anthropic 此次更新招股书，正值其筹备潜在的重磅 IPO 之际。
公司已于 10 ⽉ 26 ⽇⾸次公开提交招股书，计划在纳斯达克全球精选市场上市，股票代码为
ANT 。本次修订⽂件补充了第三季度财务数据，也进⼀步披露了公司的算⼒采购和⻓期⽀出安
排。
据此前报道， Anthropic 有望在美国感恩节假期前完成 IPO ，融资规模可能⽐肩甚⾄超过 SpaceX
今年 6 约创纪录的 862 亿美元 IPO 融资。⾼盛和摩根⼠丹利担任此次 IPO 的牵头承销商，另有 20 家
银⾏参与。
然⽽，随着最新财务数据披露，投资者对 Anthropic 估值的判断也开始出现变化。

追问：⾼速增⻓能否持续覆盖不断攀升的基础设施成本？
这也将成为 Anthropic 此次 IPO 最重要的估值考题之⼀。

## Document 286: Business Checkpoint_ Looking into the logistics Profitability growth was positive in the third quarter, but logistics costs and supply chain pressures are creating challenges. 2 mi

Source PDF: all report pdf/Business Checkpoint_ Looking into the logistics Profitability growth was positive in the third quarter, but logistics costs and supply chain pressures are creating challenges. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13028181     1

Economy 
Business Checkpoint: Looking into the logistics 
15 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Small and mid-sized businesses entered Q3 in relatively solid financial condition, with profitability growth up across both 
groups, according to Bank of America business account data. Nearly half of estimated tariff payments had been refunded by 
September, though the share of these refunds relative to revenue has peaked. 
• In Bank of America business payments data, logistics spending growth per client was up four times as much for small than mid-
sized firms. Additionally, distribution and inventory transactions growth per client has outpaced spending since the spring of 
2025. 
• Growing transportation bottlenecks are re-emerging ahead of the holiday season. Trucking capacity has tightened as driver 
availability remains constrained, according to BofA Global Research. This has pushed freight rates higher and driven strong 
transportation sector payroll growth, particularly among mid-sized businesses, according to Bank of America payments data.

Business Checkpoint is a new, regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It provides a real-time view of the financial health, opportunities 
and challenges facing small and mid-sized businesses across the US economy, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America's proprietary 
data. By analyzing activity among both small and mid-sized firms, Business Checkpoint offers timely insights into business profitability, hiring, 
investment and spending trends. Such data is not intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, 
financial condition or performance of Bank of America. 
Businesses end Q3 in fair shape 
While profitability growth remained positive for both small and mid-sized firms in the third quarter, small firms outpaced their 
mid-sized counterparts for the second consecutive quarter (Exhibit 2). Still, there’s been a notable reversal in the profitability of 
mid-sized businesses, growing this quarter after declining slightly for the past two.  
The continued rollout of tariff refunds is serving as a tailwind to some firms' cash positions. That is particularly true for small 
firms. According to Bank of America account data, by September approximately 47% of estimated International Emergency 
Economic Powers Act-related tariff payments had been refunded to companies with less than $50 million in annual revenue.

According to Bank of America business payments data, the percentage point difference in tariff refunds as a share of revenue 
for firms with less than $50 million in annual revenue spiked in June but dropped to nearly zero in September. At the same time, 
the percentage point difference in tariff payments as a share of expenses began rising again relative to the 2023 average 
(Exhibit 1). This suggests that while tariffs continue to affect companies, the refund program has provided temporary relief for 
some businesses.  
Wholesale trade along with transportation and warehousing posted solid profitability growth across business size 
However, the benefits appear uneven. Such refunds have boosted cash flow, but they have not fully insulated firms from broader 
cost pressures.  
Looking across industries, mid-sized construction and wholesale firms had the strongest profitability growth in September 
(Exhibit 3). Notably, transportation and warehousing, as well as wholesale trade were among the few sectors showing positive 
profitability growth for both small and mid-sized firms last month. 
The persistence of logistics, freight and inventory-related cost pressures may help explain why profitability gains have remained 
relatively concentrated in transportation and wholesale trade. However, these sectors also have a higher exposure to rising gas 
prices within their margins (read more on this in the August Small Business Checkpoint). 
Exhibit 3: For both small and mid-sized businesses, profitability growth was positive in transportation and warehousing as well as wholesale trade 
in September 
Profitability growth by sector and business size in September (YoY%, six-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The cost of maintaining supply chains is rising 
In fact, the prices paid component of the September services reading from the Institute for Supply Management indicates still-
building price pressure for firms (Exhibit 4). This marked the highest reading since July 2022.   
Using Bank of America internal data, we identified payments to logistics and freight companies to assess a key part of supply 
chain costs. In June, logistics and freight payments’ growth per client from small businesses grew nearly 30%, though has 
moderated to 12.7% YoY in September (Exhibit 5). Meanwhile, mid-sized firms’ payments growth in this category was up just 
3.2% YoY in September. 
The divergence suggests logistics costs may be disproportionately affecting smaller firms, who are typically less capable of 
absorbing rising costs than larger firms. Larger companies also often benefit from dedicated logistics contracts and greater 
purchasing power, while smaller ones may be more exposed to spot-market transportation costs and fluctuations in freight 
pricing.

Evidence of more moving parts in companies’ supply chains 
Yet there is increasing evidence that tariffs and geopolitics are adding complexity to small and mid-sized companies’ supply 
chains. 
In Bank of America payments data, average distribution and inventory transaction growth per business client has increased 
steadily since the spring of 2025 despite spending growth in this category falling in September (Exhibit 6). Rising transaction 
growth alongside softer spending growth suggests businesses may be engaging with more suppliers, shipments or inventory 
movements even as overall procurement outlays moderates.  
Furthermore, in May 2026, the Global Supply Chain Pressure Index reached its highest reading since the summer of 2022 and has 
continued to remain elevated into October (Exhibit 7). This further emphasizes that transportation bottlenecks, delays and 
procurement challenges are becoming more common globally. While current readings remain below the extreme levels observed 
during the pandemic, the recent move higher indicates supply chain normalization may be reversing. 
Exhibit 6: Average distribution and inventory transaction growth 
per business client has increased steadily since the spring of 2025

The trials of trucking  
As of October 1, BofA Global Research's proprietary bi-weekly Truckload Demand Indicator (see Methodology) remained below 
its historical growth signal (Exhibit 9) and the Truck Capacity Indicator, which reflects shippers' views of available capacity, 
tightened significantly, falling from 39.2 to 28.4 (Exhibit 8). This suggests shippers are seeing fewer trucks available relative to 
demand than in the previous survey. 
According to BofA Global Research, the current trucking upcycle is being driven by constrained supply rather than a significant 
improvement in freight demand. While any positive movement in demand is an important indicator as the industry moves into 
peak shipping season between late September and early December, tighter capacity appears to be the dominant force 
supporting freight rates. 
Exhibit 8: Shippers’ view of available capacity has fallen 
significantly as of October 1, 2026 
BofA Truckload Capacity Indicator by year (bi-weekly)

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 9: The Demand Indicator has been sub-60 for the past two 
months after 4.5 months above 
BofA Truckload Demand Indicator (bi-weekly)

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The reduction in capacity reflects several factors, including stricter commercial driver's license (CDL) and training requirements, 
increased compliance and enforcement measures, and other regulatory changes that have reduced the pool of qualified drivers. 
This has also likely driven some wage inflation within the industry. According to Bank of America payments data, transportation 
and warehousing payroll growth per client has surged since the start of the year, especially for mid-sized businesses (Exhibit 10).  
Having to pay higher wages suggests labor shortages remain a challenge in the sector. Firms may increasingly need to offer 
higher compensation to attract and retain workers as trucking capacity tightens. In turn, such capacity puts upward pressure on 
freight rates, particularly in the spot market where small businesses may be more impacted. 
Exhibit 10: Transportation and warehousing payroll growth has surged since the start of the year, especially for mid-sized businesses 
Payroll payments per client by industry and business size (monthly, YoY%, three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data

Logistics payments tend to increase in the second half of the year 
September marks a key run-up to the holiday season (read more on signs of holiday spending in the October Consumer 
Checkpoint), with spending on ocean shipping, containers, and other transportation services steadily accelerating ahead of the 
peak shopping period.  
In fact, over the past two years, the month of November has seen ocean shipping/container spending volumes well above their 
2023-2025 monthly average (Exhibit 11). The seasonal pattern likely reflects inventory restocking and holiday-related shipping 
activity. Ocean shipping volumes typically accelerate during the fall months as businesses prepare for year-end demand. 
Exhibit 11: Ocean shipping/container spending volumes were well above their 2023-2025 monthly average in November 
Logistics and freight average payments by category (monthly, indexed, 100 = monthly 2023-2025 average)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Distributors and inventory payments’ growth fell ahead of holiday season 
Changes in logistics and freight spending often provide an early signal of inventory trends, as distributors account for a large 
share of supply chain-related spending (Exhibit 12). 
The sharp moderation in distribution and inventory spending growth per client for both small and mid-sized businesses indicates 
that much of the large-scale inventory accumulation seen earlier in 2025 has already occurred (Exhibit 13). In other words, 
businesses appear to be replenishing inventories for seasonal demand rather than significantly expanding stockpiles as they did 
earlier in the year. 
Overall, businesses seem to be entering the holiday season in relatively stable financial condition, supported by tariff refunds 
and positive profitability growth. However, rising logistics costs, tighter trucking capacity and elevated supply chain pressures 
suggest operating conditions are becoming more challenging.

Methodology 
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business and/or Business Banking clients with a deposit account or a 
Small Business credit card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of 
America per Business client data represents activity spending from active Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card and at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and are 
generally defined as under $5mm in annual sales revenue. Mid-sized businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America 
and are generally defined as $5mm-$50mm in annual sales revenue. 
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
Revenue tiers are determined by the combination of following factors: 1) stated revenue on small businesses credit applications, 2) actual 
account inflow into Bank of America Deposit Accounts, and 3) third party revenue estimation.  
The alternative hiring indicator consists of payments from Bank of America business clients to business-focused hiring firms which include both 
direct deposits through Automated Clearing House (ACH) and payments via credit and debit cards. 
Supply chain-related spending is comprised of payments to logistics, freight, distribution, inventory, delivery service, and trucking companies. 
These are identified from merchant names and MCC codes. 
BofA Global Research’s trucking indicators are a bi-weekly diffusion-style survey of shipper demand expectations over the next 0–3 months, 
built from responses from surveyed shippers. 
Data regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -2.62 điểm tương ứng -0.15%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dệt may, Công nghệ và Máy móc là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -13,987 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã HDB, PNJ và VJC; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-30 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,267 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -2,363 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 3,726 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -2,702 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua một tuần đi ngang giằng co, giảm nhẹ và đóng cửa ở 1.735 điểm 
với thanh khoản ở mức thấp, và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài bán ròng cả 5 phiên trong tuần. 
Thông tin vĩ mô tích cực với tăng trưởng GDP quý 3 đạt mức gần 10% là điểm sáng trong 
tuần qua, tuy đã không đủ nâng đỡ thị trường chung. Thay vào đó, tâm lý thị trường thận 
trọng trước số liệu lạm phát ở mức cao, đợt tăng giá xăng dầu trong nước và mặt bằng lãi 
suất trong nước chưa có dấu hiệu hạ nhiệt. Diễn biến tăng của giá dầu thế giới, và lợi suất 
trái phiếu Chính phủ Mỹ cũng là các yếu tố gây áp lực lên thị trường chung. Tâm lý thận 
trọng còn thể hiện qua áp lực bán giải chấp ở một số cổ phiếu riêng lẻ như PNJ, SHB..., dù 
một vài nhóm trụ như dầu khí, tiêu dùng có thời điểm đóng vai trò nâng đỡ chỉ số.

Trần Đức Anh 
Giám đốc Kinh tế Vĩ mô & Chiến lược thị trường 
anhtd@kbsec.com.vn 
07/10/2026

Triển vọng TTCK 4Q2026 
Tích lũy trong vùng nhiễu động

Chúng tôi dự báo vùng điểm hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm 2026 
xuống 1,800 đi ểm, giảm mạnh từ mức 1,950 đi ểm đưa ra trong báo cáo gần nhất. 
Vùng điểm này tương ứng:

- Tăng trưởng EPS 2026 so với cùng kỳ của thị trường chung ở mức 33% 
(tăng mạnh so với mức 25% của báo cáo gần nhất), chủ yếu nhờ kỳ vọng 
vào các chính sách thúc đẩy kinh tế của Chính phủ, và lợi nhuận tăng 
trưởng đột biến ở VIC (xem thêm phần Triển vọng hoạt động kinh doanh).

- Mức P/E hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm ở mức 13.2 (giảm 
nhẹ so với mức 13.5 thời điểm hiện tại - phản ánh quan điểm các yếu tố 
rủi ro sẽ tiếp tục duy trì). Đây là mức định giá thấp so với bình quân 5 năm 
gần nhất ở 15.1. Nếu loại bỏ yếu tố đột biến ở nhóm Vingroup, chỉ số P/E 
điều chỉnh hiện đã rơi xuống 10.9, thấp hơn các đáy gần nhất năm 2020, 
2022 và 2025. Trong bối cảnh kinh tế Việt Nam duy trì tốc độ tăng trưởng 
cao, lợi nhuận các doanh nghiệp niêm yết liên tục bứt phá, vùng định giá 
này mở ra cơ hội đầu tư trung/dài hạn. Chúng tôi cho rằng ngay khi các rủi 
ro vĩ mô quốc tế (xoay quanh vấn đề Iran), và trong nước (xoay quanh vấn 
đề lãi suất) hạ nhiệt, thị trường chung sẽ có cơ hội tái định giá lên mức cao 
hơn đáng kể so với thời điểm hiện tại. Dù vậy, khác với báo cáo gần nhất 
vào tháng 7, ở báo cáo này, chúng tôi đẩy lui thời điểm các yếu tố rủi ro vĩ 
mô hạ nhiệt sang năm 2027, đồng nghĩa với quan điểm thận trọng hơn về 
thị trường cho quý 4 2026.

Cụ thể, đối với vấn đề lãi suất, áp lực lạm phát lớn dần, đi kèm nhu cầu vốn cao phục 
vụ hoạt động đầu tư cơ sở hạ tầng sẽ khiến mặt bằng lãi suất duy trì ở mức cao cho 
phần còn lại của năm 2026 (dù thanh khoản hệ thống ghi nhận sự cải thiện đáng kể 
trong 1 quý gần đây). Đối với vấn đề xung đột Trung Đông, các cuộc đàm phán vẫn 
đang bế tắc và ít có kh ả năng dòng ch ảy dầu mỏ tại khu vực này được nối lại ngay 
trong năm nay.

Chủ đề đầu tư chúng tôi l ựa chọn cho 4Q2026 là đ ầu tư công, chính sách ti ền tệ hỗ 
trợ, và xung đột Iran.

Xem đầy đủ tại đây.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành mẫu nến Doji trên khung đồ thị tuần sau hai tuần giảm điểm liên tiếp, 
cho thấy áp lực bán đã phần nào được kiểm soát. Trên khung ngày, nỗ lực hồi phục đầu 
tuần gặp lực cản tại đường SMA50 khiến VNINDEX quay đầu giảm điểm, tiếp tục chớm phá 
đáy ngắn hạn; tuy nhiên vùng hỗ trợ gần đã tạo hiệu ứng thu hút lực cầu bắt đáy, giúp chỉ 
số thu hẹp đà giảm trong phiên cuối tuần. Sau nhịp điều chỉnh khá tiêu cực từ vùng đỉnh 
đầu tháng 9, VNINDEX đang bước vào giai đoạn tìm kiếm điểm cân bằng trở lại và diễn biến 
giằng co điều chỉnh có thể còn kéo dài thêm trong các phiên kế tiếp. Tuy nhiên khi xu 
hướng tăng ngắn hạn tính từ vùng đáy cuối tháng 7 vẫn đang được bảo lưu, chỉ số được kỳ 
vọng sẽ có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại tại điểm đỡ gần quanh 1710 (đây cũng là chốt chặn cần 
giữ để bảo vệ được xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn).

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years. 1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.

4 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.

## Document 337: Eurozone_ Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer confidence falls back (2026-10-02)

Eurozone: Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer

confidence falls back
02/10/2026 3
We have selected two inflation measures (with and without energy) and six survey indicators: 1/ & 2/ business confidence, as
measured by the PMIs in the manufacturing and services sectors; 3/ & 4/ input and output prices components of the
composite PMI (in order to identify direct inflationary pressures); 5/ the suppliers’ delivery times component of the
manufacturing PMI (a direct indicator of possible supply difficulties and supply-demand imbalance, and therefore, indirectly,

of inflationary pressures in the making); and 6/ consumer confidence (measured by the “assessment of financial situation in
the next 12 months” in order to capture the impact of inflation on purchasing power). The trends in each of these indicators
are observed relative to month m=0, corresponding to the start of the conflict. Each line does not represent the level of the
indicator, but its cumulative variation compared to month m=0.
The overall picture from the confidence surveys available for September is globally positive. According to the PMIs, the

business climate in services has indeed seen a clear improvement, and that in the manufacturing sector remains comfortably
in expansion territory for the 8 th month in a row. However, consumer confidence has recorded its first decline in 5 months,
likely driven by rising inflation and concerns over purchasing power. News on the inflation front is negative. PMI surveys
report a strengthening of inflationary pressures: this trend is not a surprise given the tensions in energy prices, but it is

nonetheless significant. As for inflation itself, it continues to rise, more markedly in September: +0.6 percentage points, to
3.8% year-on-year (Eurostat flash estimate), of which 0.4 pp du to the higher energy contribution. Inflation excluding energy
increased as well, by 0.2 pp to 2.3% (of which 0.1 pp due to the rise in food inflation).
Source: S&P Global, Eurostat, European Commission, Macrobond, BNP Paribas

## Document 344: FOMC 明確さを待つために様子見

Source PDF: all report pdf/FOMC　明確さを待つために様子見.pdf
Report source: DIR

Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

米国

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9 番 1 号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー

2026 年 10 月 29 日 全5 頁

FOMC 明確さを待つために様子見 
FOMC はインフレ高止まり、景気下振れ、金融面のリスクに直面

経済調査部 主任研究員 矢作 大祐 
ニューヨークリサーチセンター 研究員 藤原 翼

[要約] 
 2026 年10 月のFOMC では、FF 金利の誘導目標レンジを3.75～4.00％に据え置いた。市
場は事前にFF 金利の据え置きを織り込んでおり、決定後の長期金利、株価、ドルにも
大きな変動は見られなかった。 
 声明文では景気判断を上方修正し、ウォーシュFRB 議長も FOMC 後の記者会見で、個人
消費、設備投資、雇用環境の底堅さを強調した。そして、インフレ率は依然高すぎる
ことも引き続き強調し た。他方、金融環境に関するウォーシュ議長の指摘には変化が
見られた。資本配分の選別が進み、一部資産では信用スプレッドが急拡大するなど、
金融環境には引き締まりの兆候が表れているとした。 
 もっとも、ウォーシュ議長は金融環境の引き締まりの 程度を断定するのは時期尚早と
の見解を示した。ウォーシュ議長は今後のデータによって状況がより「明確」になる
と述べ、フォワードガイダンスの提示には慎重な姿勢を維持しつつも、12月のFOMC で
利上げに踏み切る可能性をにじませたと解釈すべきである。 
 9 月時点のFOMC 参加者の金利見通しと直近のFedWatch は、12 月のFOMC での0.25％pt
追加利上げを示唆している。しかし、実施の可否は予断を許さない。注目点は、期待
インフレ率の上振れ、雇用統計、とりわけ労働参加率の改善、社債利回りなどに表れ
る金融ストレスの 3 点である。FOMC は、利上げによる景気・金融環境の悪化と、据え
置きによるインフレ期待の上振れというジレンマに直面しており、政策判断とともに
丁寧な情報発信が求められる。

### Page 2

1

2 / 5

政策金利の据え置きを決定 
2026 年 10 月 27 日・28 日に開催された FOMC（連邦公開市場委員会）では、政策金利である
FF（フェデラルファンド）レートの誘導目標レンジを 3.75～4.00％に据え置いた。なお、今回
の決定について、クックFRB 理事とクリーブランド連銀のハマック総裁が0.25％pt の利上げを
主張して反対票を投じた。 
今回の決定は、市場にとってサプライズとはならなかった。CME が算出する FedWatch（FF 先
物市場から算出される利上げ・利下げ確率）によると、FOMC の開催直前（10 月 26 日）の時点
で、金利据え置きの確率が約 90％となっていた。前回（2026 年 9 月 15 日・16 日）の FOMC で
0.25％pt の利上げが実施された後、FOMC 参加者は根強いインフレを背景に年内の追加利上げを
主張する一方、利上げの時期に関しては慎重に評価していくとの見解を示していたことから、
市場も金利据え置きを織り込んでいた。 
なお、FOMC 後の記者会見において、ウォーシュ FRB 議長は来年以降における FOMC の開催ス
ケジュールに言及した。具体的には、今後も 1 年あたり合計 8 回の会合を行う一方で、そのう
ち 2027 年は 1 回分、2028 年は 2 回分を金融政策の広範な戦略に特化した会合に充てるとし、
金融政策の決定や声明文の公表は行わないと発表した。

声明文・記者会見：金融環境の変化を指摘する一方、慎重に評価を行う意向 
今回公表された声明文を見ると、冒頭で「委員会は10 対2 の賛成多数で以下の声明の公表を
承認した」と前置きした上 で、「委員会は、連邦準備制度の二重の目標（デュアルマンデート）
を支えるため、FF 金利の目標レンジを3.75～4.00％に維持することを決定した。委員会は、銀
行システムにおける潤沢な準備預金を維持する政策を継続する」と決定内容を示した。 
続いて、景気認識については、「経済活動は力強く拡大している。地政学の動向などにより
不確実性は依然として高いものの、国内支出は底堅く推移している。生産性の伸びは力強く、
設備投資も堅調だ」と、前回の「経済活動は堅調に拡大」から「経済活動は力強く拡大」に上
方修正された。雇用環境については、 「雇用者数の増加は労働力人口の増加に追いついており、
失業率はほとんど変化していない」との表現が据え置かれた。ウォーシュ議長は記者会見で、
2026 年上半期の実質GDP 成長率が上方修正され、第3 四半期も力強い成長が見込まれると指摘
した。さらに、個人消費は堅調で、設備投資は、けん引役となっているハイテク関連だけでは
なく、それ以外の項目にも波及が見られると述べた。他方でウォーシュ議長は、多くのFOMC 参
加者は景気が過熱しているとは評価しておらず、その要因として 、自身の見立てでは生産性が
改善していることに言及した。また、雇用環境については、新規失業保険申請件数が低水準で
推移していると指摘し、極めて良好と評価した。 
物価関連の記述を見ると、 「インフレ率は依然として高水準にある。本日の政策措置は、委
員会が掲げる 2％目標への早期回帰を支援するものである。委員会は物価安定を実現する」と

### Page 3

1

3 / 5

の表現から「インフレ率は依然として高水準にある。より多くの情報が集積されることで、委
員会はインフレ率を目標の 2％に速やかに戻すための適切な政策スタンスを決定できるだろう。
委員会は物価安定を実現する」に変更された。ウォーシュ議長は記者会見で、インフレ率は依
然として高すぎると指摘した。さらに、直近数週間においてエネルギー価格やそれに関連する
価格が再上昇していることに警戒感を示した。もっとも、ウォーシュ議長は FRB の取り組みを
評価する指標として期待インフレ率を挙げ、現時点では正しい方向に進んでいるとの認識を示
した。 
また、今回の記者会見では、金融環境についての質問が目立った。ウォーシュ議長は、一部
の企業や家計において金融環境は引き締まりつつあるとの見解を示し、 市場参加者は資本配分
について以前よりも慎重かつ選別的な姿勢をやや強めていると言及した。足元で注目される AI
関連投資の資金調達を含め、金利は幅広い分野に直接・間接の影響を及ぼしているとの認識を
示した。9 月初め以降、市場参加者が投資対象をより選別するようになり、一部の資産では信
用スプレッドが急拡大し、その他の資産でも、金融環境は大幅ではないものの、幾分引き締ま
っていると指摘した。 
もっとも、ウォーシュ議長は、金融環境は引き締まりつつあるものの、現時点で「引き締ま
った状態にある」と判断するのは時期尚早との見方を示した。その上で、今後得られるデータ
によって状況がより「明確（more clarity ）」になると述べた。 この「明確」という表現は、
様々な情報を把握するという目的で様子見を決定した今回のFOMC とは対照的な表現と捉えるべ
きだろう。とりわけ、ウォーシュ議長はフォワードガイダンスを示すことに慎重な姿勢を続け
ていることを踏まえれば、自身のそうした姿勢と矛盾しない範囲で、12 月の FOMC で利上げに
踏み切る姿勢をにじませたといえるのではないか。

FOMC はインフレ高止まり、景気下振れ、金融面のリスクに直面 
今回のFOMC では利上げをせず、様子見となった。市場でも据え置きが想定されていたことか
ら、予想通りの結果といえる。10 年債金利や主要株価指数、ドル指数などにも大きな変動は見
られなかった。 
直近のCPI、PPI が市場予想を上回るなどインフレ懸念が強まる中でも、様子見となった背景
には、景気の下振れリスクへの警戒感があるだろう。ウォーシュ議長は景気を堅調と表現した
が、経済指標を見ると、9 月の雇用統計の雇用者数や小売売上高に減速感が見られるなど、屋
台骨である個人消費には下振れリスクがある。また、けん引役の設備投資も CoreWeave 問題な
どを背景に、債務の持続可能性が警戒されており、 社債利回りは上昇している 1。金融環境全
体に関して、OFR（米財務省金融リサーチ室） の金融ストレス指数に注目すると、足元で上昇
（ストレスの強まり）傾向が見られる（図表 1 左図）。内訳を見ると、ボラティリティ要因がプ

1 矢作大祐・藤原翼「米国経済見通し CoreWeave 問題をどうみるか」（大和総研レポート、2026 年10 月 22
日）

### Page 4

1

4 / 5

ラスに転じ、クレジット要因のマイナス幅も縮小している。金融環境にストレスが見られつつ
ある中で、FOMC 参加者は様子見姿勢を強めたと考えられる。 
では、先行きの金融政策はどう考えればよいか。9月のFOMC で公表された、FOMC 参加者のFF
金利見通し（中央値）では、2026 年12 月末時点でFF 金利は4.1％となっている。10 月のFOMC
でFF 金利が据え置かれたことから、FOMC 参加者の予想通りであれば、12 月のFOMC での0.25％
pt 追加利上げが想定される。なお、10 月 29 日時点の FedWatch でも 12 月の FOMC では 0.25％
pt の利上げ確率が高いと予想されている。また、ウォーシュ議長が FOMC 後の記者会見で今後
得られるデータによって状況がより明確になると述べたことについて、利上げの可能性をにじ
ませたと解釈すべきとしたのは前述の通りだ。 
図表 1 金融ストレス指数、期待インフレ率の期間構造

（注）左図の金融ストレス指数は上昇すると、金融ストレスの強まりを示す。 
（出所）OFR、クリーブランド連銀、Haver Analytics より大和総研作成

他方で、実際に 12 月の FOMC で利上げを実施できるか否かは予断を許さない。利上げの可能
性を考える上では、3 つの注目点を取り上げたい。1 つ目の注目点として、インフレ率の高止ま
りリスクに関連して、期待インフレ率に注目したい。ウォーシュ議長はFOMC 後の記者会見で、
現時点で期待インフレ率が正しい方向に向かっていると評価したが、クリーブランド連銀が算
出した期待インフレ率の期間構造を見ると、2026 年 10 月は 2026 年 9 月に比べて上方シフトし
た（図表1 右図）。期待インフレ率の上方シフトがさらに進めば、アンカリング機能が低下して

じた。労働需給や賃金上昇圧力の緩和だけでなく、消費者の雇用マインドの下支えという意味

る。こうした状況では、政策判断そのものに加え、市場の利上げ 期待の過度な変動を招かない
よう、FOMC 参加者の丁寧なコミュニケーションも重要となろう。

## Document 347: FX Daily_ Dollar holds gains into midterms

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Daily_ Dollar holds gains into midterms.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

At the start of a very busy week for FX markets, the dollar is
largely holding onto recent gains. In focus are the US midterms,
where betting markets attach a 70% probability to a clean
sweep for the Democrats. Away from politics, we'll receive
updates on the US jobs market this week and are expecting
rate hikes in the UK, Australia and Sweden

Chris Turner, Frantisek Taborsky and Francesco Pesole
The US midterm elections are set to dominate a busy week in FX. A clean
sweep for the Democrats is expected, but the macro story can keep the dollar
supported
USD: Contemplating the midterms
The dollar is fractionally lower at the start of the week. It may be
taking its cue from oil prices, which are oﬀ 2-3% on reports that US
President Donald Trump wants to let diplomacy run its course with
Iran. That reverses Friday's sentiment, where some had positioned
for a fresh attack this weekend. Please see our latest thoughts on
the oil market here.

### Page 2

In focus this week are the US midterm elections. Betting websites
give the Democrats a 70-72% chance of taking both the House and
the Senate. One could make the case that the dollar should come
lower on such an outcome, since it would likely lead to gridlock on
the debt ceiling, a prolonged government shutdown next year and
could encourage President Trump to pursue an even more activist
foreign policy agenda. Many of those views are speculative,
however. What is not speculative is the US economy posting a

strong 3.4% quarter-on-quarter annualised growth rate in the third
quarter, inﬂation remaining above target for a sixth year in a row,
and the Federal Reserve readying to tighten again.
Feeding into the US macro debate this week will be ISM business
surveys and jobs data. Business surveys are expected to show
continued optimism, along with high price pressures. And Friday's
October jobs report is expected to show a modest rebound after a
soft September ﬁgure. Barring a wholly disastrous report on Friday,

we doubt the jobs data really moves the needle on the Fed
tightening story. Price data is more important here.
There will be continued focus on the tech sell-oﬀ, however. Apple
reports after the market close today. The 9% Nasdaq sell-oﬀ last
month has driven a softening in short-dated US rates, and any
further large sell-oﬀ in this sector is probably the biggest risk to
long dollar positioning.
Expect DXY to continue trading around 102.00, and we do not see a

strong case for a correction lower.
Chris Turner
EUR: Fitch puts the boot in
Unwelcome news for French politicians late Friday came in the form
of an unscheduled ratings review from Fitch. It shifted its outlook on
French sovereign debt from stable to negative and promised
another ratings review by the end of February. It currently rates
France as A+. Reasons for the outlook shift are familiar by now, but
they centre on the government's failure to improve public ﬁnances

at a time of weak growth. Let's see whether this unscheduled move

GBP: 25bp BoE rate hike fully priced for
Thursday
Sterling is trading reasonably well. Helping it has been the view that

high energy prices leave the Bank of England no alternative but to
hike rates on Thursday. A 25bp rate hike to 4.00% looks like a done
deal. Upside economic surprises, UK ﬁscal policy seemingly
heading in the right direction and warmer ties with the EU all seem
to be helping sterling too. Notably, speculators are still running
reasonably large short positions in sterling, which suggests this
period of sterling outperformance can continue.
EUR/GBP has come a long way very quickly – a near 4% drop in a

little over a month. Some consolidation is probably due. But with
high energy prices likely to keep expectations of higher UK policy
rates in place until early next year and French budgetary problems
unresolved, it is too early to expect a signiﬁcant low in EUR/GBP.
Chris Turner

With the start of a new month, the CEE narrative is becoming more
attractive once again. Today, PMI data for the region will be

released. Since the onset of the US-Iran conﬂict, these ﬁgures have
either remained stagnant or trended upward, indicating that the
economies are currently showing strong resilience to the energy
shock.

Tomorrow, we will see Turkey’s inﬂation ﬁgures for October. Our

meetings. The combination of rising energy prices and a stronger
dollar globally, alongside generally dovish central banks within the
CEE region, will continue to exert pressure on local currencies.

has returned to the 24.300–350 range, which we consider fair for

CNB, the bar is high given that 100bp of tightening is already priced
in and the koruna has a tendency to weaken slightly following CNB
meetings. We could see the pair move slightly above 24.350 again.

## Document 350: Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Jan Hatzius 
+1(212)902-0394 | jan.hatzius@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC
Global Views: Downside Growth Risks
26 October 2026 | 6:45AM EDT
1. So far in 2026, global growth has been remarkably unremarkable.  Both the world 
as a whole and the G3 economies are growing in line with our estimates of potential 
GDP .  Our interpretation is that fiscal easing and the positive impulse from the AI 
boom have offset the negative impulse from the war in Iran and the lingering drag 
from higher US tariffs.

2. Our standing global GDP forecasts project more of the same in 2027, as lower 
energy prices offset fading tailwinds from fiscal policy and AI.  But the balance of 
risks is on the downside.  The most immediate concern is that, far from subsiding, 
energy prices have again climbed sharply on fears of renewed Middle East escalation.  
The prices of gasoline, diesel, jet fuel, and European/Asian natural gas are now near 
or even above their March/April highs, with likely negative effects on consumers and 
energy-importing economies more broadly.

### Page 2

3. Although we don’t expect significant fiscal policy changes in 2027, the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—have 
started to show up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates.  The moves have 
been mostly orderly, but the increase since early 2026 averages a sizable 100bp across 
DM yield curves.  If sustained, we estimate that this increase will subtract a cumulative 
0.6% from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment.  
(Only part of the drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect bond 
yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.)

Exhibit 2: Oil Price Risks Are Skewed to the Upside

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 3

4. The growth impulse from the AI boom was set to peak in late 2026 or early 2027 even 
before concerns around the sustainability of the AI investment boom intensified in 
recent weeks.  Although AI investment is still rising rapidly, we expect the increase as a 
share of GDP to slow over the next year.  Alongside the fading boost from last year’s 
increase in US depreciation allowances, this will weigh on capital spending growth, 
especially in the US.  AI has also boosted US consumer spending growth via a positive 
wealth effect worth an estimated 0.4pp (or 0.25% of GDP) over the past year.  This 
impact is likely to decline over the next year, gradually if stocks perform in line with our 
strategists’ reasonably upbeat expectations but more sharply if AI-related stocks 
continue to decline.

### Page 4

5. Our own base case remains that AI investment will ultimately pay off in terms of higher 
productivity and economywide profits.  However, one key channel for this payoff is labor 
market disruption, which has negative near-term macroeconomic effects.  And while DM 
labor markets still look solid in aggregate, we are starting to see clearer signs that the AI 
impact is spreading beyond the technology sector.  Across nonmanufacturing industries, 
the share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment.

6. The Fed’s recent signals—explicit from Williams/Jefferson and implicit from Warsh—
effectively rule out a hike on October 28.  But the FOMC still looks very likely to deliver 
another 25bp hike to 4-4.25% on December 9 because spot inflation remains too high.  
And while we continue to expect significant disinflation next year on the back of 
reduced impulses from tariffs, energy prices, and AI-related prices, the risks to our 
forecast are on the upside, most obviously in energy.  Higher inflation could result in

another hike in March, but either way we see renewed cuts in late 2027/early 2028.

7. Like the Fed, the ECB will likely hold rates steady next week and deliver a final hike (to 
2.75%) at the December meeting.  However, the turbulence in the French bond market 
poses somewhat greater downside risks to that call than in the US.  The turbulence has 
tightened financial conditions to a degree that is likely to shave ¼-½pp from annualized 
growth in coming quarters.  And if it intensifies into December, markets might perceive a 
hike as signaling insufficient focus by the ECB on the risk of Euro area fragmentation.  
While the ECB could address this issue by coupling a hike with renewed balance sheet 
expansion, the hurdle for such a move is high, in part because purchases of French 
bonds would inevitably raise concerns about moral hazard with respect to the needed 
fiscal adjustment.

### Page 6

November 5.  Consistent with our view that the UK economy is stronger than widely 
perceived, GDP growth has surprised on the upside this year and the unemployment rate 
declined to 4.7% in August on a 3-month average basis (with the volatile 1-month rate 
plunging to 4.1%).  Rising inflation and the looming household energy price reset in 
January imply a significant risk of further moves, although market pricing of 4+ hikes 
(including November) still looks excessive.  We also expect hikes in Canada and New 
Zealand this week and in Australia next week.

9. Slightly faster than expected growth in Q3 has pushed up our 2026 GDP forecast for 
China to 4.6%.  But most activity indicators are much weaker.  Both property activity and 
fixed asset investment are contracting year-on-year, and while nominal retail sales 
growth picked up to +1.1% in September, large retailers (whose sales are relatively 
better measured) are still posting substantial year-on-year declines.  With domestic 
demand rising slowly at best, sustained GDP growth in the official 4.5-5% target range 
will require ongoing steep increases in the trade surplus.  But the surplus is already at 1% 
of global GDP , and further increases will eat more noticeably into output and 
employment abroad.  Ultimately, China’s trading partners may respond by imposing 
trade barriers that weigh on Chinese exports and GDP , although the measures taken so 
far have been limited.

Exhibit 8: UK Growth Forecasts Have Drifted Higher

Source: Bloomberg, Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   6
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 7

10. Our strongest global view is that central banks will deliver fewer hikes than currently 
priced.  In addition, our rates strategists flag underweight positioning, diminished 
technical risks, and risk asset pressures as reasons why the recent rally might extend.  
The market’s narrow breadth makes our equity strategists wary of momentum factor 
exposure, but they still expect the S&P 500 to resume its climb after the midterm 
election, as earnings continue to grow solidly.  Our credit strategists remain cautious on 
IG technology bonds but see scope for some near-term reversal in HY given the scale of 
the recent selloff.  Our FX strategists expect the Japanese yen to act as a hedge against 
further AI jitters, in part because such jitters would shrink US-Japan interest rate 
differentials.  And our commodity strategists recommend targeted exposures to oil 
products and European natural gas to hedge near-term escalation risks alongside 
longer-term exposures to copper, gold, and select power markets to benefit from 
structural supply/demand imbalances. 
Jan Hatzius

存儲記憶體⼤漲價造成的  PC 需求不振，外加以⾊列廠爆炸停⼯影響
數據中⼼晶⽚⽣產，造成  Intel 四季度營收，⽑利率及 EPS 都不如市

ANDREW LU ON GLOBAL SEMI/TECHS

READ IN APP
Great improvement in 3Q26

Intel reports 3Q26 sales of US$16.5bn, 2% qoq and 21% yoy, in line
with Bloomberg estimates. 3Q26 gross margin of 43% was 2ppts
higher than Bloomberg estimates. 3Q26 EPS of US$0.6 is much
higher than Bloomberg’s US$0.28 largely due to 2ppts better gross
margin, 4ppts better operating margin and non-operating income of
US$1.1bn.
Data center IC sales beat: 3Q26 Data center IC sales (largely
server CPU sales) were US$7.32bn, up 17% qoq and 78% yoy,
nearly 10% above consensus estimates. Data center IC sales

accounted for 44% of 3Q26 sales, up from 2Q26’s 39% and from
3Q25’s 30%. Operating margin for data center IC sales was 42%,
much higher than Bloomberg’s 27%, and improved from 2Q26’s
40% and 3Q25’s 23%.
Improve in progress for foundry business: In addition to
stronger data center IC sales, Intel foundry 3Q26 sales improved to
US$6.7bn, up 16% qoq and 58% yoy. Driven by higher utilization
rate, 3Q26 operating margin for Intel foundry was improved to -28%

### Page 2

from 2Q26’s -36% and 3Q25’s -55%, which was much better than
Bloomberg’s -36% estimates.
Source: Intel, Bloomberg est.

### Page 3

Source: Andrew Lu on global semis/techs
4Q26 guidance missing on sales, margin, and EPS
Combined eﬀects from lack of demand on client computing (due to
memory price hike and PC customers to aggressively manage
inventory might extend to 1H27) and lack of data center CPU
capacity (limited by in house capacity after Israel Kiryat Gat fab
factory shut down), Intel guides 4Q26 sales of US$15-16bn, down 3-
9% qoq and 9% below Bloomberg estimate of US$17bn (Fab shut

down aﬀects 2ppts sales). 4Q26 gross margin is guided to
deteriorate to 37% (Fab shut down aﬀects 2ppts margin), 6ppts
lower than 3Q26’s 43% and Bloomberg’s 42.8%, resulting much
lower diluted EPS of US$0.1 (Bloomberg’s US$0.3).

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 22 日

1/3 
NZ ドル相場の行方を左右する総選挙と RBNZ の政策運営 
～インフレ圧力は根強い一方、政権交代に伴う政策枠組みの変更が不透明要因に～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ニュージーランドでは、インフレ高進を背景に RBNZ が９月に２会合連続の利上げを実施し
たものの、11 月７日の総選挙を巡る不透明感がＮＺドル相場の重しとなってきた。 新興政党
のオポチュニティ党が連立協議のカギを握る可能性が高まっているうえ、仮に左派・労働党
を中心とする政権への交代が実現すれば、RBNZ の政策目標に雇用の最大化が再び加えられる
と見込まれる。そのため、追加利上げのハードルが高まる可能性が意識されている。 
○ ７-９月のインフレ率は前年比＋3.7％と鈍化し、市場予想も下回ったものの、引き続き目標
レンジを上回っている。エネルギーや食料品を中心にインフレ圧力が後退する一方、サービ
ス物価には上昇圧力が広がる兆しがうかがえる。さらに、原油価格の高止まりや干ばつによ
る食料インフレの懸念も残る。金融市場では早期利上げ観測がくすぶるものの、今回の物価
統計を受けて見方が修正される可能性もあり、先行きのＮＺドル相場は総選挙の結果と、そ
の後のRBNZ の政策枠組みに関する議論に左右される展開が予想される。

【RBNZは追加利上げ実施も、総選挙を巡る不透明感がNZドル相場の重しとなる展開が続いた】 
ニュージーランドでは、RBNZ（ニュージーランド準備銀行）が９月の定例理事会において２会合
連続の利上げを決定した （注１） 。 背景には、2025 年以降のインフレ率が加速してRBNZ の定める目
標レンジ（１～３％）の上限を上回る伸びで推移していることがある。さらに、イラン情勢の悪化
による原油価格の上昇がエネルギーインフレを招き、４-６月のインフレ率は前年比＋4.1％と2023
年 10-12 月以来の高い伸びとなった。こうした事情は、RBNZ の断続的な利上げ実施を後押ししたと
考えられる。 
一方、通貨ＮＺドル相場は上値の重い展開をみせてきた。同国では、11 月７日に議会（代議院：
一院制 （総議席数120） ） の総選挙の実施が予定されており、選挙結果を巡る不透明感が影響してい
るとみられる。世論調査によれば、右派の国民党、ないし左派の労働党を中心とする連立政権がい
ずれも多数派を形成できず、新興政党・オポチュニティ党がキャスティングボートを握る可能性が

注１ ９月２日付レポート「RBNZ は緩やかな引き締め示唆､NZ ドル相場の行方はどうなる？」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/654752.html）

### Page 2

2/3 
浮上している（注２） 。 同党は、都市部で年間1.75％、 農村部で0.5％の土地税を新設し、 それを財
源の一部に原則18 歳以上の国民に年間1.94 万ＮＺドルのベーシックインカム （ＢＩ） に相当する
「市民所得」を支給する税制改正を公約に掲げる。国民党、労働党ともに同党が掲げる税制改正案
の採用を否定しており、実現のハードルは高い。しかし、それ以外の政策の方向性については、左
派陣営と比較的親和性が高いとみられるため、選挙後に行われる連立協議の行方が注目される。 
こうしたなか、政治的な争点のひとつにRBNZ（ニュージーランド準備銀行）の政策運営が浮上し
ていることも注目される。労働党を中心
とする左派陣営 は、RBNZ のマンデート
（政策目標）の変更を公約に掲げてお

見込まれる。足元の雇用環境は、直近４

ため、RBNZ のブレマン総裁は９月会合後
の記者会見において、選挙結果は政策決
定に影響しないとの見方を示したもの
の、追加利上げのハードルが高まることは避けられない。こうした事情も、ＮＺドル相場の重しに
なってきたと考えられる。

【金融市場はRBNZの早期利上げを意識するも、NZドル相場の行方は総選挙の結果がカギを握る】 
このため、金融市場においては、RBNZ
の政策運営を占ううえで 物価動向に注
目が集まった。７-９月の消費者物価は
前年比＋ 3.7％に伸びが鈍化したもの
の、 ５四半期連続で目標レンジを上回る
推移となった（図２）。ただし、 この水準
は RBNZ が９月会合に際して示した見通
し（前年比＋3.9％）を下回るとともに、
事前の市場予想をも下回る伸びにとど
まった。前期比も＋0.66％と前期 （同＋
1.49％）から上昇ペースが鈍化してお
り、 原油価格の上昇一服を受けてガソリンなどエネルギー価格が下落したほか、 生鮮品をはじめと

ーインフレが長引く可能性が高まっている。さらに、エルニーニョ現象の発生を受けて、同国では
東部や北部を中心に、乾燥・干ばつによる農業生産への悪影響が懸念されており、食料インフレや
景気の下押しが懸念される状況にある。 
総選挙においてはインフレも主要な争
点となっており、早期のインフレ抑制に
向けて、RBNZ が前倒しで利上げに動くと

ってきた可能性がある （図３） 。今回の物

けて、 早期利上げ観測が修正される可能
性もある。先行きのＮＺドル相場の行方
を巡っては、総選挙の結果と、その後の
RBNZ の政策枠組みに関する議論に左右
される可能性にも注意が必要と考えられる。

## Document 497: 【國泰證期研究部】被動元件產業-VPD供電驅動Embedded MLCC，電容由板上走進基板-20261027

Source PDF: all report pdf/【國泰證期研究部】被動元件產業-VPD供電驅動Embedded MLCC，電容由板上走進基板-20261027.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1 
產業更新 – 被動元件產業 
VPD驅動Embedded MLCC，電容由板上走進基板 
What’s New - 垂直供電推動MLCC埋入基板 
AI加速器的耗電持續攀升 ， 供電設計由主機板側邊的水平供電 ， 轉向把電源模組直接擺在晶片底
下的垂直供電(VPD)。晶 片 底 下 可 用 空 間 極 小，MLCC只能做得更薄 、 改埋進基板內部 ，Embedded 
MLCC因此從驗證走向量產導入。三星電機10/21宣布與NVIDIA合作供應AI晶片封裝載板，
載板中放入其自家生產的MLCC，MLCC內埋載板也將於2H26出貨給新的AI客戶；太陽誘電
的可埋入MLCC已於25年8月量產 ， 國巨已建立可依客戶需求客製尺寸的CE系列embedded 
MLCC產品線，村田則看好相關營收自27年起隨客戶導入逐步發酵。 
Trends/Outlook - VPD改變MLCC用量結構，27年放量導入 
展望27~28年，我們認為VPD與IVR普及後，晶片底下以表面貼裝擺放的MLCC顆數增速將
放緩，但MLCC價值會往埋在基板裡的高規格產品集中，這類產品在外部電極平整度、背面電
極精度與薄型化上的門檻遠高於一般品；此外，加速器所需的供電仍有一半以上走傳統水平路
徑，主機板上的高容MLCC用量不會因此被取代。預期Embedded MLCC將於27年後隨客戶
導入逐步放量 ， 具備薄型化與電極精度控制能力的日韓大廠將率先受惠 ， 國巨則具備產品基礎 。

Investment Summary 
1. Embedded MLCC是VPD架構帶出的MLCC新應用。三星電機手握MLCC與封裝載板兩項
能力，又於10/21與NVIDIA展開AI晶片封裝載板合作，載板中即放入自製MLCC；村田持
續優化埋入用MLCC規格，看好27年起營收開始貢獻；太陽誘電22µF可埋入MLCC已於25
年8月量產。我們認為VPD會讓MLCC由主機板上大量鋪貼的標準品，轉為埋入基板的高附
加價值產品，具備薄型化與電極控制技術的大廠可望掌握價值轉移。 
2. 國巨(2327 TT)近期仍以板端高容MLCC受惠日韓大廠產能轉向高階後的轉單與漲價為主要
支撐；公司亦已推出厚度僅0.2mm、尺寸可依客戶需求客製的CE系列embedded MLCC，我
們將Embedded MLCC視為國巨中長期的成長關鍵商品之一，維持買進評等。

產業報告 
 2026/10/27

2 
垂直供電改變AI晶片供電路徑，MLCC由板上移入基板 
AI加速器的工作電壓只有0.7~1.2V，耗電卻一路逼近千瓦，電壓越低、電流越大，電力在傳遞
途中的損失與電壓起伏就越難控制。過去主流的水平供電，把降壓用的電源模組擺在主機板上處
理器旁邊，電流得沿著板面繞一段路才送進晶片，耗損隨距離放大。VPD的做法則是把最後一級
電源轉換模組搬到處理器正下方或主機板另一面，讓電流直接由下往上穿過板子送進晶片，大電
流行經的路徑因此大幅縮短，既減少損耗，也騰出處理器四周的空間。更進一步的整合式電壓調
節器(IVR)，則直接在處理器封裝內完成最後一次降壓，把電壓降到晶片使用的1V以下。

當電源模組移到晶片正下方，原本擺在處理器底下的去耦電容只能變得更薄，並改放進基板或封
裝裡面。Embedded MLCC便是把MLCC直接藏進晶片下方的基板層中，電容與晶片的距離拉
近後，寄生電感隨之下降，高頻雜訊也能壓低；另一種landside MLCC則貼在封裝底面，善用
PCB背面原本閒置的面積。對照太陽誘電規劃的電源架構，主機板供電、VPD與IVR三種方案
都會用到埋入基板的MLCC，其中VPD還需搭配高容值MLCC擔任輸入與輸出端電容，以及低
ESL MLCC。三 星 電 機 目 前 針 對VPD評估中的去耦MLCC包含X7T 0402 22µF與X6S 2.5V 47µF
兩款，顯示VPD偏好小尺寸、高容值MLCC。

圖一. 太陽誘電AI伺服器電源架構與MLCC需求

資料來源: TAIYO YUDEN、國泰證期研究部整理

要埋進基板的MLCC，規格和板面貼裝品不同：元件夾在基板各層之間，靠導通孔與線路接通，
外部電極必須做得極為平整，埋入後接點才不鬆脫 ，厚度也得大幅壓縮 。進度上 ，太陽誘電1005
尺寸(1.0mm×0.5mm)、容值22µF的可埋入MLCC，25年8月已投產，樣品每顆20日圓；國
巨CE系列embedded MLCC的代表規格為0402尺寸、X5R、6.3V、100nF，厚度0.2mm；
村田則同時研發可埋入基板的MLCC與矽電容，並備有多種電極設計。

### Page 3

投資 顧問 
產業報告 
 2026/10/27

3 
Embedded MLCC商業化關鍵：連接可靠度與製程門檻 
我們認為Embedded MLCC能否放量，關鍵不在技術能否實現，而在埋入後的接點能否長期穩
定。MLCC原為表面貼裝而設計 ， 改成埋入式後 ， 電極平整度 、 背面電極的精準度都需重新調整 ，
日系大廠正以累積多年的品質數據為基礎，改良相關規格以符合伺服器的要求。若與同樣能埋入
基板的矽電容比較，矽電容的焊墊做法讓接點較穩固，價格卻明顯偏高；MLCC補足接點可靠度
的短板，憑藉成本優勢可望在埋入市場勝出。另外，埋入式方案並不會全面取代傳統配置，即使
VPD與IVR的採用率提高 ， 加速器所需電力仍有過半維持水平供電 ， 板上MLCC需求依舊存在 。

表1. 板端MLCC、Embedded MLCC與矽電容比較 
資料來源：國泰證期研究部整理

從時間軸來看，Embedded MLCC已經走過產品開發階段，開始進入少量導入： 太陽誘電可埋入
MLCC於25年8月投產，三星電機MLCC內埋載板將自2H26交貨給新AI客戶，村田預估相
關營收26年貢獻有限、27年逐步拉升。我們預期VPD在27~28年將從少數高階設計往更多平
台擴散，晶片底下表面貼裝MLCC的顆數成長會放慢，然而埋在基板內的高規格MLCC，以及
VPD模組周邊所需的高容值MLCC將形成新的需求，MLCC的價值將由顆數轉向規格。封裝內
的電容並非只有Embedded MLCC一種選擇。矽電容可依需求放在晶片旁(T op-side)、矽中介
層或封裝底部(Land-side)，或 直 接 嵌 入 封 裝 基 板 與 增 層 之 中，三 種 位 置 都 比 主 機 板 端 更 貼 近 晶 片
核心：擺在晶片旁的矽電容負責吸收最高頻的電流變化，嵌入基板者則縮短電源進入封裝後的第
一段路徑，與Embedded MLCC的位置最為接近，兩者在基板內的應用空間部分重疊。 
項目 板端MLCC Embedded MLCC 矽電容 
製程 陶瓷介質多層堆疊燒結 陶瓷MLCC薄型品 半導體晶圓製程(光刻、蝕刻) 
放置位置 PCB板端、電源模組 封裝基板內、VPD模組 晶片旁、矽中介層、基板內 
容值 高 高 nF~μF等級 
ESL 較高 較板端低 極低

4 
圖二. 矽電容可放置主要區域圖 
資料來源: Samsung Electro-Mechanics、TrendForce、國泰證期研究部整理

供給面上，Embedded MLCC需要MLCC廠與載板廠一起完成驗證，而能做內埋元件的高階載
板本身就相當稀缺。對於被動大廠而言，大客戶對多層核心、內含元件的高階載板需求升溫，但
兼具技術與量產能力的供應商屈指可數。三星電機在越南擴充AI封裝用的內埋載板產線，台灣
載板業者目前尚未公開內埋MLCC的相關進度，我們認為同時掌握MLCC與載板技術的業者，
在共同驗證與良率掌控上占有先機。

日韓大廠領先布局，國巨具備產品基礎 
我們認為Embedded MLCC正在由少量導入轉向放量的階段。三星電機擁有MLCC與封裝載板
兩項能力，可一次提供完整方案；10/21與NVIDIA展開策略合作，供應AI晶片封裝載板並合
資建置專用產線，載板中即放入三星電機自製的MLCC。此外，VPD用的去耦MLCC已進入客
戶評估階段，MLCC內埋載板也將自2H26起出貨給新的AI客戶。

村田為MLCC龍頭，同時發展embedded MLCC與矽電容，並針對埋入用途優化電極平整度與
背面電極精度、準備多種電極設計，生產規劃也會依埋入式與一般外掛式電容的需求消長彈性調
配；客戶對Embedded Capacitor的洽詢持續增加，惟26年營收貢獻仍小，預期27年起逐步
放大。太陽誘電則已量產22µF可埋入MLCC，並把基板內埋MLCC定為AI伺服器重點產品。

國巨(2327 TT)近期的主要獲利動能，仍主要來自日韓大廠將產能移往高階產品後，中低階訂單
轉向國巨所帶來的量價齊揚；Embedded MLCC方面，國巨已推出0402尺寸、厚度0.2mm的
CE系列embedded MLCC，尺寸可依客戶需求客製，具備卡位基板內埋市場的條件。我們將
Embedded MLCC視為國巨中長期的成長關鍵商品之一，維持買進評等。

## Document 578: 今月のグラフ（2026年10月）制度変更が押し上げた耐久財需要

Source PDF: all report pdf/今月のグラフ（2026年10月）制度変更が押し上げた耐久財需要.pdf
Report source: MURC

Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

自動車
エアコン
（2020年 = 100）
（年、月）
2026 年 10 月 5 日

調査レポート

今月のグラフ（2026 年 10 月）

制度変更が押し上げた耐久財需要

調査部 研究員 小林 啓介

2026 年 4～6 月期の実質 GDP における個人消費は前期比＋0.0%と横ばいにとどまったものの、耐久財は同
＋4.0%と堅調に増加した。特に自動車とエアコンの需要が大きく高まったことが、押し上げに寄与したとみられる。 
新車販売台数（除く軽）は、自動車の燃費性能に応じて取得時に課税される「環境性能割」が 2025 年度末に
廃止されることを受けて今年度に需要の一部が先送りされた結果、4 月に前月比＋25.7%と急増した。同様にエ
アコンの出荷台数も、2027 年度から省エネ基準が厳しくなり、製品価格の上昇が予想されることから、それ を見
越した駆け込み需要が生じ、4 月には前月比＋12.5%と急増した。 
もっとも、こうした制度・規制変更に起因する一時的な需要増は徐々に落ち着きつつある。自動車とエアコンは
いずれも依然として高水準ではあるものの、4 月をピークに減少傾向に転じている。雇用・所得環境は良好であり、
イラン情勢悪化の影響で落ち込んでいた 消費者マインドも持ち直しへ向かっているなど、 個人消費を取り巻く環
境は悪くない。このため、7～9 月期の個人消費は底堅く推移するとみられるものの、耐久財消費の一時的な押し
上げ効果が剥落することで、小幅な伸びにとどまる可能性があることには注意を要しよう。

図表 新車販売台数（除く軽）とエアコン出荷台数

会「家庭用（ルーム）エアコン国内出荷実績」
